# China

Several terms redirect here. For the present-day Republic of China, see [Taiwan](25734.md). For 中国 in Japan, see [Chūgoku region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C5%ABgoku_region). For other uses, see [China (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_\(disambiguation\)) and [PRC (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PRC_\(disambiguation\)).

**China**,[^cite_note-20] officially the **People's Republic of China** (**PRC**),[^cite_note-21] is a country in [East Asia](19605700.md). It is divided into 33 [province-level divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province-level_divisions_of_China), including two [special administrative regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_administrative_regions_of_China).[^cite_note-23] [Beijing](18603746.md) is the capital, while [Shanghai](27643.md) is the [most populous city by urban area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_China_by_population). Its geography features the [Central Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongyuan), major rivers such as the [Yangtze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangtze) and [Yellow River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_River), deserts, [subtropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subtropical) and temperate forests, plateaus, and [mountain ranges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_range) such as the [Himalayas](37966.md). It is the [world's second-most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population_\(United_Nations\)) after [India](14533.md), with [a population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_of_China) exceeding 1.4 billion (17% of the world's population). Covering 9.6 million square kilometers (3,700,000 sq mi), it is the [third largest country by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area).[^cite_note-area-24]

Humans first arrived in China during the [Paleolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleolithic). During the 2nd millennium BCE, [dynastic states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynasties_in_Chinese_history) emerged. In 221 BCE, China was unified under the [Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_dynasty), followed by the [Han dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_dynasty), ushering in two millennia of imperial rule across periods of unity and division. The [imperial era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_era_of_Chinese_history) saw the expansion of the [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road) and the invention of [gunpowder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invention_of_gunpowder), [paper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_paper#Paper_in_China), [printing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Printing), and the [compass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compass). After increased Western political, economic, and philosophical influence, the empire was succeeded by the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)) (ROC) after the [1911 Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1911_Revolution). The [Warlord Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warlord_Era) and [Chinese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War) followed, interrupted by the [Second Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Sino-Japanese_War), which ended in a Chinese victory. In 1949, the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) (CCP) [proclaimed the PRC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) and the [ROC retreated to Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retreat_of_the_government_of_the_Republic_of_China_to_Taiwan). Both sides still claim political legitimacy. Attempts by the CCP to advance [its political program](46636.md) faltered through [famine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Chinese_Famine) and political turmoil. The [reform and opening-up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_and_opening_up) that began in 1978 moved China towards a [socialist market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_market_economy), spurring economic growth.

China is a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) with the CCP as its [sole ruling party](102459.md). It is one of the five [permanent members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_members_of_the_United_Nations_Security_Council) of the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) and a member of [many multilateral organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_organization_membership_of_China). China is the world's [largest manufacturer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_manufacturing_output) and has the [second-largest economy by nominal GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)). International organizations have criticized China, alleging authoritarianism and [human rights abuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China). Possessing [a large military](66890.md), [space program](343674.md), and [nuclear stockpile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_China), China has been described as a [superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower) due to its influence in geopolitics, [science and technology](277914.md), [manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_in_China), [economics](19284336.md), and [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_culture).

[Chinese art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_art) and culture have [influenced much of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinosphere), with [a literary tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_literature) dating back millennia to the [Chinese classics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_classics). China is the birthplace of [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism) and [Daoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daoism), which form the traditional [three teachings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_teachings) of [Chinese philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_philosophy) and [folk religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_folk_religion) alongside [Buddhism](3267529.md). [Chinese cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_cuisine) is diverse and highly regional, with rice as a staple in the south and wheat in the north. It has [60 World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_China), including the [Great Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China) and [Grand Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Canal_\(China\)). The [Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese), speakers of [Sinitic languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinitic_languages), are China's dominant ethnicity, although it is home to [55 recognized minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ethnic_groups_in_China), including the [Hui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hui_people), [Mongols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongols), [Tibetans](249458.md), [Uyghurs](31783.md), and [Zhuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuang_people).

## Etymology

Main article: [Names of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_China)

The word "China" has been used in English since the 16th century; however, it was not used by the Chinese themselves during this period. Its origin has been traced through [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_language), [Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_language), and [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language) back to the [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit) word *Cīna* (चिन), used in [ancient India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_India).[^cite_note-OED-25] *Cīna* was first used in early [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) scriptures dating from the 3rd century BCE to 4th century CE, including the *[Mahabharata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabharata)* and the *[Laws of Manu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manusmriti)*. In 1655, the missionary [Martino Martini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martino_Martini) suggested that the word China is derived ultimately from the ancient state of [Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_\(state\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWade200920-26] This remains a common etymology.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWade200920-26][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodde198620-27] Another possible source is the ancient [Guizhou](173815.md) polity of [Yelang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yelang), known as *ʐina* in [Loloish languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loloish_languages).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWade200920-26]

The official name of the modern state is the "People's Republic of China" (中华人民共和国; *Zhōnghuá Rénmín Gònghéguó*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEda_Graça2000224-28] The shorter form of this name is 中国; *Zhōngguó*, from *zhōng* ('central') and *guó* ('state'), a term first used for the [demesne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_land) of the [Western Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Zhou) dynasty.[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChen2004198-29][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012191–193-31] The names of ruling imperial dynasties were typically used to refer to the state and its territories. In the 1800s, *Zhongguo* was officially adopted as the name of the country by the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012191–193-31]

## History

Main article: [History of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_China)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Chinese history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Chinese_history).

### Prehistory

Main article: [Prehistory of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistory_of_China)

Evidence of early humans such as *[Homo erectus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_erectus)* in China dates to the [Paleolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleolithic), around 1.7 million years ago, with unconfirmed sites as old as 2 million years ago. Modern *[Homo sapiens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homo_sapiens)* are attested from around 50,000 years ago.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi201521,_37–39-32] Following the end of the [Last Glacial Period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_Glacial_Period), in about 8,000 BCE, pottery-making [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) cultures emerged. By the 6000s BCE, sedentary agricultural societies had spread across the lower [Yellow River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_River) basin.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012662-33][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi201550-34] These societies became increasingly complex, urbanized, and stratified,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi2015127-35] but many experienced a population collapse in the late 2000s BCE for unclear reasons.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi2015164–167-36]

### Ancient China

A set of bronze ritual vessels arranged in a museum display

A set of [Western Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Zhou)-era [ritual bronzes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_ritual_bronzes), c. 1000 BCE

After 2000 BCE, a [Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze_Age) culture emerged in the [Central Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongyuan), dubbed the [Erlitou culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erlitou_culture) after its [main site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erlitou). Erlitou has been controversially identified with the [Xia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xia_dynasty), the traditionally-accepted first [dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynasties_of_China), but no written records exist to confirm this.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi2015162–167-37][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201022–23-38][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012678–679-39] The [Shang dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shang_dynasty) purportedly succeeded the Xia around 1600 BCE, with its early stages tentatively identified with an expansionist state known archaeologically as the [Erligang culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erligang_culture).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi2015196–198-40] The historicity of the [Late Shang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Shang) is attested through divinational writings in the [oracle bone script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_bone_script). These are the earliest known form of writing in China and the ancestor of modern [Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_characters).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPuett201694–95-41][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199712-42]

The Shang were overthrown by the [Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhou_dynasty) c. 1046 BCE. The Zhou ruled over a vast and loose confederation of vassal states across central China, which was gradually weakened by regional lords.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi2015264–266-43] Centralized authority collapsed in 771 BCE, giving way to constant regional warfare. During the [Eastern Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Zhou), the multitude of small polities of the [Spring and Autumn period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_and_Autumn_period) evolved into seven territorial [Warring States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warring_States_period).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi2015266–267-44][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201038–39-45] Literary and philosophical developments of this period include the emergence of various schools of thought, such as [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism), [Daoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daoism), [Mohism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohism), and [Legalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legalism_\(Chinese_philosophy\)), alongside [Chinese classics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_classics) like the Confucian *[Analects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Analects)* and the Daoist *[Dao De Jing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tao_Te_Ching)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199726-46][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolcombe201736,_40–41-47]

### Imperial China

Further information: [Chinese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Empire)

#### Classical period

A group of the Terracotta Warriors standing in an archaeological dig site

Soldier statues of the [Terracotta Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terracotta_Army), buried alongside [Qin Shi Huang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_Shi_Huang) c. 210 BCE

In the 220s BCE, [Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_\(state\)) rapidly conquered the other warring states. In 221, its ruler [Qin Shi Huang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_Shi_Huang) proclaimed himself the first [emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_of_China) (皇帝; *Huángdì*) and founded the [Qin dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_dynasty).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201060–61-48][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodde198640,_53–55-49] He led an [autocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autocracy) Legalist state organized in a system of [commanderies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commandery_\(China\)) and [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_\(China\)). The dynasty lasted only fifteen years, falling soon after his death from [revolts and civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chu%E2%80%93Han_Contention).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201061–63-50][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodde198654–56-51]

After the collapse, the [Han dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_dynasty) emerged to rule China between 206 BCE and 220 CE.[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012704-52] The Han gradually reinstated centralized control, legitimizing their rule through Confucian scholarship and political institutions which would be maintained in some form into the 20th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201024–28,_36-54][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201064–66-55] Military expeditions against the [Xiongnu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiongnu), a confederation of nomadic [steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Steppe) tribes, expanded Han influence into parts of [Central Asia](6742.md) and helped to establish the [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road), allowing for trade connections between China and western Eurasia. Contemporaneously, merchants established [maritime trade routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Silk_Road) linking China, [Southeast Asia](28741.md), and [India](14533.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201024–28-56][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201068–70-57] The dynasty is the namesake of the [Han Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese), China's main ethnic group.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolcombe201750-58]

The Han faced widespread uprisings and the emergence of local warlords in the 100s and 200s CE.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201084-59] By 220, the empire was split into the [Three Kingdoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Kingdoms).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolcombe201756–58-60] These were briefly united by the [Jin dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(266%E2%80%93420\)) in 280, which fell into civil war. [Sinicized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinicization) formerly-nomadic peoples who had settled in Northern China, such as the Xiongnu, rebelled and [founded new dynasties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixteen_Kingdoms).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolcombe201760–63-61][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201037–38-62] [Various Han Chinese dynasties in Southern China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_and_Southern_dynasties#Southern_dynasties) emerged in the 300s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolcombe201763–65-63] During these conflicts, [Buddhism](3267529.md) was introduced to China via the Silk Road.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201096–98-64][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201042–45-65]

#### Mid-imperial period

China was reunited under the [Sui dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sui_dynasty) in 589,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201090–94,_108-66] which constructed the [Grand Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Canal_\(China\)) to link Northern and Southern China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010114–116-67] The Sui collapsed in the 610s, and was succeeded by the [Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_dynasty). The Tang dynasty centralized the state and dispatched military expeditions to pacify the surrounding regions. It promoted [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Buddhism), but through expanding international trade built a heavily cosmopolitan society centered on its capital of [Chang'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang'an).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201052–56-68] It reformed the [civil service examinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_examination) and oversaw a flourishing of art, poetry, architecture, and scholarship.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESullivan1984114–117-69][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010112,_120–121-70][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201063-71] The 755–763 [An Lushan rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_Lushan_rebellion) weakened the Tang, which gradually fragmented before collapsing completely in 907, resulting in [another period of war and division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Dynasties_and_Ten_Kingdoms_period).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010127–130,_136-72][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201066–67-73]

A section of a Song dynasty painting showing a boat nearly crashing into a bridge

Section from the [Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_dynasty)-era landscape painting, *[Along the River During the Qingming Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Along_the_River_During_the_Qingming_Festival)*, early 1100s

The [Song dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_dynasty) rose to power in 960. It faced military crises, unable to subdue its Sinicized non-Han neighbors, the [Khitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khitan_people)-led [Liao dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liao_dynasty) and the [Tangut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangut_people)-led [Western Xia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Xia). The Song's consistent focus on defense resulted in a heavily centralized state and military, which made the first military application of [gunpowder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gunpowder).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010136–138-74][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201067–69-75] Books became widely available by the proliferation of [printing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Printing) technology,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010152-76] while the elite class of [scholar-officials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scholar-official) grew increasingly powerful.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010149-77] Production, population, and trade expanded massively, alongside innovations such as industrial [metallurgy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metallurgy) and [hydraulic machinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydraulic_machinery).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010141–144-78] The Song capital [Kaifeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaifeng) was overrun by the [Jurchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurchen_people)-led [Jin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_dynasty_\(1115%E2%80%931234\)) in 1127, forcing the Song to retreat to Southern China. During this period, the revivalist philosophical movement of [Neo-Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Confucianism) emerged.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201073–74-79]

#### Late imperial period



A [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty) section of the [Great Wall of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China)

In 1206, [Genghis Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genghis_Khan) united the nomadic [Mongols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongols) to the north under the [Mongol Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_Empire). Over the following fifty years, they [conquered the Western Xia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_conquest_of_Western_Xia) and [the Jin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_conquest_of_the_Jin_dynasty), in addition to [their other conquests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_conquests) as far west as Europe. [The empire split](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_of_the_Mongol_Empire) into separate khanates, with Mongol leader [Kublai Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kublai_Khan) establishing the [Yuan dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty) and [subjugating the Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_conquest_of_the_Song_dynasty) in the 1270s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201079–81-80][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010169-81] Although Chinese culture remained largely unchanged, the Yuan maintained a Mongol elite culture and established a complex ethnic and occupational hierarchy. Foreign presence increased as China became more connected to Eurasian trade routes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010173–176,_183-82]

In the 1340s, central China was devastated by disease, famine, and severe flooding along the Yellow River. [Various rebellions erupted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Turban_Rebellions), leading to a peasant leader establishing the [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty) in 1368 and declaring himself the [Hongwu Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongwu_Emperor).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201083–86-83][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010190–191-84] The Ming later moved their capital from [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing) to [Beijing](18603746.md), with the [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City) as the imperial palace. However, the lower Yangtze remained the wealthiest region. Trade grew, and the [European colonization of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_colonization_of_the_Americas) brought new crops and a massive influx of silver to China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201087–91-85] The population doubled, and a growing publishing industry began producing works in [vernacular Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Written_vernacular_Chinese), including the [Four Classic Novels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_Chinese_Novels).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201091–92-86]

The Ming state declined, and by the 1600s, became unable to contain [peasant rebellions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Ming_peasant_rebellions). The [Manchus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchu_people) to the north declared the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) in 1636 and [conquered the Ming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transition_from_Ming_to_Qing),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp201094–96-87] killing their last claimant emperor in 1662 and conquering a [Ming rump state on Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tungning) in 1683.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERowe200925–27-88] From the late 1600s to the end of the 1700s, the [High Qing era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Qing_era) saw economic growth and territorial expansion westward, including the [conquest of Tibet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_expedition_to_Tibet_\(1720\)) and much of [Central Asia](6742.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERowe200997–100-89][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERowe200963-90][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010227-91]

An oil painting of European and Chinese ships docked at a trading port

1805 painting of the [European factories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Factories) in [Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou) (Canton)

European powers fought various wars against the Qing dynasty during the 19th century. These began with the United Kingdom's [First Opium War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Opium_War) in 1839–1842,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010236-92][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERowe2009170–172-93] which resulted in the first of the "[unequal treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unequal_treaties)" imposed on the dynasty. These opened [treaty ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_ports), allowed Christian missionary activity, and loosened trade restrictions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010240-94] China faced an economic crisis and internal unrest, and uprisings such as the 1850–1864 [Taiping Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiping_Rebellion) resulted in the deaths of millions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010106–107-95][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010243-96] Reformist factions of the Qing responded with the [Self-Strengthening Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-Strengthening_Movement), seeking to adopt Western weapons and technologies, but this had a limited impact for much of the empire.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010108-97]

The [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan)'s defeat of the Qing in the [First Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Sino-Japanese_War) (1894–1895) resulted in the loss of [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Prefecture) and the growth of both reformist and revolutionary political movements. An [imperial push for reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hundred_Days'_Reform) in 1898 ended after an internal coup, while the anti-foreign [Boxer Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxer_Rebellion) was defeated by a [coalition of foreign powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eight-Nation_Alliance) in 1901. The late Qing dynasty again [advanced reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Qing_reforms), but growing revolutionary and anti-Manchu sentiment culminated in the [1911 Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1911_Revolution). A coalition of revolutionaries led by [Sun Yat-sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Yat-sen) overthrew the Qing and declared the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)) (ROC) in 1912.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010111–115-98][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010265-99]

### Republic of China

Main article: [Republic of China (1912–1949)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\))

A black and white postcard showing the Shanghai Bund, with streetcars and European-style buildings

[The Shanghai Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund), c. 1934

President [Yuan Shikai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_Shikai) rose to power in 1912 and crushed his main opposition, the [Kuomintang](16903.md), ruling China as a dictator until his death in 1916, following an abortive attempt to restore the monarchy. During the [Warlord Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warlord_Era), feuding regional warlords and governors took power across China, while Tibet and [Mongolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogd_Khanate_of_Mongolia) declared independence. The nominal republican government had little control outside Beijing. During this period, [New Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Culture_Movement) intellectuals and students criticized traditional society. A crackdown on a student protest in 1919 led to the [May Fourth Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Fourth_Movement) and further agitation by intellectuals for a cultural and political upheaval, with many embracing Western political ideas such as [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010115–118-100][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010267–272-101]



[Mao Zedong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mao_Zedong) and [Chiang Kai-shek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiang_Kai-shek) toasting together in 1945 following the [end of World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_World_War_II_in_Asia)

In the mid-1920s, the Kuomintang [allied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_United_Front) with the nascent [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) (CCP), and partially reunified China under [Chiang Kai-shek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiang_Kai-shek)'s leadership. Starting with the [Shanghai massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_massacre) in 1927, the Kuomintang violently purged the CCP and gained the allegiance of the northern warlords, establishing a new government at Nanjing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010120–122-102] The CCP was driven into the countryside and [repressed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Terror_\(China\)), but subsequently [regrouped in the northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_March).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010275-103][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010127–128-104]

Japan [occupied Manchuria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_invasion_of_Manchuria) in 1931, and launched an [invasion of the rest of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Sino-Japanese_War) in 1937. A [renewed coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_United_Front) between the CCP and Kuomintang fought Japan in what became a theater of [World War II](32927.md), as Japanese forces committed numerous [war atrocities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_war_crimes) against the civilian population and occupied most of China's major cities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010129–130-105][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010285-106][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZarrow2006295-107] After the [surrender of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrender_of_Japan) in 1945, China became a founding member of the [United Nations](31769.md) and regained control over Manchuria and Taiwan. The [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War) between the CCP and the Kuomintang resumed the following year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZarrow2006296–298-108]

### People's Republic of China

Main article: [History of the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China)

After a string of military victories, CCP chairman [Mao Zedong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mao_Zedong) formally [proclaimed the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) (PRC) in 1949. The ROC government [retreated to Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retreat_of_the_government_of_the_Republic_of_China_to_Taiwan) and continued to claim legitimacy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpence2013456–459-109] The following year, the PRC [occupied and annexed Tibet](14615536.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010303-110] The PRC, which the CCP described as a "[people's democratic dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_democratic_dictatorship)", enacted many reforms: it restructured the economy under state control, promoted literacy and gender equality, and expanded heavy industry. The [Land Reform Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_Reform_Movement), in which the state encouraged and organized peasant violence against landlords, leading to the death of upwards of a million people, had begun in northern China during the civil war and extended to most of the rest of China between 1950 and 1952. It distributed landlord land to the peasants as private property, but it was soon followed by collectivization of the land.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpence2013464-111][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010294–298-112][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010139,_142,_146-113]

A group of pedestrians and cyclists by a large poster with Chinese political writing, alongside a portrait of Mao Zedong and a Chinese flag

A [big-character poster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big-character_poster) and portrait of [Mao Zedong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mao_Zedong) in Beijing during the [Cultural Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Revolution), 1976

In 1958, Mao launched the [Great Leap Forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Leap_Forward), a mass industrialization project which resulted in the [Great Chinese Famine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Chinese_Famine) and millions of deaths. As the [Cold War](325329.md) deepened, China [grew politically isolated from its former ally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Soviet_split), the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), as well as the [Western Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Bloc). It [detonated its first atomic bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_596) in 1964.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010139–141-114][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010310–313-115] Attempting to reassert control over the CCP after the Great Leap, Mao and his allies launched the [Cultural Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Revolution) in 1966, sparking a decade of political violence, crackdowns on perceived counterrevolutionaries, and social upheaval that lasted until [Mao's death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Mao_Zedong) in 1976.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010314–319-116][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010143–145-117]

[Deng Xiaoping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deng_Xiaoping) served as [paramount leader](262766.md) from 1978 to 1989. The [reform and opening-up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_and_opening_up) policies of the 1980s and 1990s saw economic liberalization towards a [socialist market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_market_economy), rapid economic growth, and improved relations with the West. [Political dissent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protest_and_dissent_in_China) culminated in a mild crackdown early in 1987 and a much more severe and prolonged crackdown in 1989 that included the [Tiananmen Square massacre](44534.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERopp2010147–151-118] China shifted away from an agricultural economy and rapidly urbanized, although this led to stark [economic inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_inequality_in_China) between urban and rural areas, as well as environmental deterioration.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010344–349-119] Since the 1990s, market forces have become China's main economic driver.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGilley2024134–136-120] Under the [general secretaryship of Xi Jinping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_secretaryship_of_Xi_Jinping) since 2012, he has launched a far-reaching [anti-corruption campaign](45655698.md), expanded Chinese economic influence through the [Belt and Road Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_and_Road_Initiative), and abolished [term limits](505825.md) for himself as [state president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_China).[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2024141,_149–154-122]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_China)

Further information: [Climate of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_China) and [Geology of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_China)

Tall, cone-like and lush green mountains rising up from a flooded river valley

Mountains along the [Li River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_River) in [Guangxi](47836950.md)

Old ruins on a hillside surrounded by dry, arid mountains

[Subashi Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subashi_Temple) ruins in the [Taklamakan Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taklamakan_Desert), [Xinjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang)

A mountain range covered in a forest of trees at sunset

Boreal forest in the [Greater Khingan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Khingan), [Inner Mongolia](55064.md)

China's geography is highly varied, featuring a [dry and mountainous west](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_China), alongside both mountains and wide river valleys in the east. Chinese civilization was traditionally centered in the lowlands around China's two longest rivers: the [Yangtze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangtze) and the [Yellow River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_River), both originating on the [Tibetan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_Plateau). This temperate region is bordered by forest and [steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe) to the north, as well as the mountainous and subtropical [south](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_and_southern_China).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShelach-Lavi20159–10-123] China's shoreline spans the [Bohai Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohai_Sea), [Yellow Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_Sea), [East China Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_China_Sea), and [South China Sea](74209.md), all part of the [Pacific Ocean](23070.md). Its largest island is [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Taiwan), and the largest governed by the PRC is [Hainan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hainan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXuChenXu201847-124] [Poyang Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poyang_Lake) is the largest of the country's many freshwater lakes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXuChenXu201846-125]

Large mountain ranges in the west, including the [Himalayas](37966.md) and [Tian Shan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian_Shan), separate China from [South](21566765.md) and [Central Asia](6742.md). China features both the [Turpan Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turpan_Depression), among the deepest points on land, and the high Tibetan Plateau.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVeeck_et_al.201621–22,_31-126] The world's highest point, [Mount Everest](42179.md) (8,848 metres or 29,029 feet above [sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level)), lies on the border with [Nepal](171166.md).[^cite_note-127] China has a wide variety of ore and mineral resources.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXuChenXu201844–45-128]

China's climate is dominated by [dry seasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_season) and wet [monsoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsoons), which lead to pronounced seasonal temperature differences. In the winter, northern winds coming from high-latitude areas are cold and dry; in summer, southern winds from coastal areas at lower latitudes are warm and moist.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFu_et_al.20081-129] The northwestern deserts receive as little as 50 mm (2.0 in) of rainfall annually, while much of southern China exceeds 1,000 mm (39 in).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVeeck_et_al.201624–25-130] Eastern China is well-suited for agriculture, with two or three harvests per year. The south is dominated by [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) farming, while the north grows crops such as [wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat) and [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVeeck_et_al.2016243–244-131]

China frequently [faces natural disasters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_disasters_in_China). Its adjacency to both the [Pacific Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_of_Fire) and the [Alpide belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpide_belt) (tectonic belts) results in frequent earthquakes, the cause of the majority of China's natural disaster deaths. Severe quakes such as the [1976 Tangshan earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Tangshan_earthquake) cause hundreds of thousands of deaths.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWangLiangShi2022231–232-132] Rainstorms and flooding are common hazards,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWangLiangShi2022234–236-133] with the Yellow River's perennial floods devastating the surrounding plains throughout recorded history.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiuChen201228-134] [Typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon) frequently impact southeastern China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWangLiangShi2022241–243-135]

### Environment

Main articles: [Environment of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_China), [Wildlife of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_China), and [Environmental issues in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_China)

See also: [Renewable energy in China](13556279.md), [Energy policy of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_policy_of_China), and [Climate change in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_China)



A [giant panda](12713.md), a symbol of China, eating [bamboo](39029.md)

China is one of 17 [megadiverse countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries), containing a wide variety of forests, [shrublands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrubland), wetlands, [steppes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe), and deserts, which host (as of 2018) 92,300 known species and subspecies of plants, animals, and fungi.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGIZ202213–14-136] According to government surveys, China's forest coverage grew from 10% of the overall territory in 1949 to 25% in 2024.[^cite_note-137] The far north contains [taiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiga), which transitions into [deciduous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deciduous) forest to the south. South of the Yangtze, mixed conifer and deciduous forest is common, with tropical and subtropical forests farther south.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVeeck_et_al.201639–40-138]

In the 21st century, China has suffered from [environmental deterioration and pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_China) due to rapid development, alongside the broader effects of [climate change](5042951.md). About 11% of its plant species and 21% of its [vertebrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertebrate) species are threatened or endangered due to [habitat loss](1707053.md), pollution, and [poaching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poaching).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGIZ202214–15-139] "Flagship" endangered species such as the [giant panda](12713.md) and [tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger) have received additional funding and protection.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuang_et_al.20211-140]

Although China has the world's highest [CO<sub>2</sub> emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_carbon_dioxide_emissions), it only emits 8 tons of [CO<sub>2</sub> per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_carbon_dioxide_emissions_per_capita) as of 2020, significantly lower than many developed countries.[^cite_note-UCS-2020-141] Total [greenhouse gas emissions by China](61886740.md) are the [world's largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_greenhouse_gas_emissions).[^cite_note-UCS-2020-141] China has prioritized reducing pollution,[^cite_note-142] announcing its aims to reach its peak emissions levels before 2030, and achieve [carbon neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_neutrality) by 2060.[^cite_note-CAT-2020-143][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2024150-144] [Water quality in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_pollution_in_China) has been impacted by rapid development and high levels of [agricultural runoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_pollution) and [heavy metal pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_metal_pollution).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFeng_et_al.20212-145]

China is the world's leading investor in [renewable energy](25784.md) and [its commercialization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_commercialization), investing CN¥3.6 trillion (US\$546 billion) in 2022 alone.[^cite_note-Schonhardt-2023-146] Traditionally reliant on non-renewable energy sources such as [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_in_China), China's adoption of [renewable energy](13556279.md) has increased significantly in the 2010s and 2020s.[^cite_note-147] In 2025, 54.4% of China's electricity came from coal, while 41.6% came from clean energy, with the remainder from other [fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel).[^cite_note-148] Despite its emphasis on renewables, China remains dependent on global oil markets.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu_et_al.20251-149]

### Political geography

Main articles: [Borders of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borders_of_China), [Coastline of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastline_of_China), [Territorial changes of the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_changes_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China), and [Territorial disputes of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_disputes_of_China)



China's administrative divisions and territorial disputes

A map showing territorial disputes in the South China Sea. Various contrasting blue lines are shown corresponding to each country, with dozens of green islands labeled as disputed.

Territorial disputes in the [South China Sea](74209.md), with the Chinese claim in red

China is the [second-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area) country by land area after [Russia](25391.md), and the third or fourth largest by total area.[^cite_note-area-24] Total area figures are generally around 9,596,961 km<sup>2</sup> (3,705,407 sq mi).[^cite_note-UN_Stat-8][^cite_note-CIA-12] China [borders 14 nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borders_of_China), with a combined land border length of 22,117 km (13,743 mi). Its [coastline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastline_of_China) spans approximately 14,500 km (9,000 mi).[^cite_note-CIA-12]

The PRC has engaged in 23 border disputes since 1949, of which six remain unsettled.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFravel20081,_2-150] It actively [disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Indian_border_dispute) several portions of its Himalayan border with India, including [Aksai Chin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aksai_Chin) in [Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir) and the Indian state of [Arunachal Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arunachal_Pradesh), which led to [military skirmishes in 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%932021_China%E2%80%93India_skirmishes).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVerma2024397–398-151][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFravel2008321–334-152] It maintains maritime disputes with Japan [over the Senkaku/Diaoyu Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senkaku_Islands_dispute) and with various countries over a group of [South China Sea Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Sea_Islands) within the "[Nine-dash line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nine-dash_line)", including the [Spratlys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spratly_Islands) and [Paracels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paracel_Islands). This has led to [skirmishes with Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Vietnamese_conflicts_\(1979%E2%80%931991\)) and the [Philippines](23440.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFravel2008321–334-152][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthgate2019163–165-153]

Both the PRC and the ROC on Taiwan claim legitimacy as the government of China, leading to [cross-strait tensions](2596399.md). Taiwan is a [*de jure* province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_\(People's_Republic_of_China_province\)) of the PRC,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2020213-154][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao202346-22][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChen20221029–1033-155] with the ROC-controlled islands of [Kinmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinmen) and [Matsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matsu_Islands) as a part of [Fujian Province](88585.md).[^cite_note-asia-34729538-156][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChang201535–40,_46–60-157]

## Politics

Main article: [Politics of China](23239.md)



The [Great Hall of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Hall_of_the_People),\
where the [National People's Congress](38878740.md) convenes



[Zhongnanhai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhongnanhai), headquarters of the [Chinese government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Council_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) and [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md)

China is a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) under the [absolute leadership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Party_leads_everything) of the Chinese Communist Party. Since the 1980s, the CCP has called its guiding theories and policies [socialism with Chinese characteristics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_with_Chinese_characteristics), which it considers an adapted form of [Marxism–Leninism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism) that emphasizes a market economy as the [primary stage of socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_stage_of_socialism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024b189–190-158][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFang2020v–vi-159][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPearsonRithmireTsai202347–48-160] China describes itself as a [socialist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_state_\(communism\)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPearsonRithmireTsai202347–48-160][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024210-161] and a [people's democratic dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_democratic_dictatorship).[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFang2020xv-164] Many academic sources describe it as an [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) state,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024a5-165][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERingen2016138-166][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELaikwan20241-167] although others dispute this label as reductive,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELaikwan20241-167] or as oversimplifying its methods of political control.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERingen2016138-166]

The [National People's Congress](38878740.md) (NPC) holds the [unified powers of the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unified_state_power) and oversight over all state organs, with its [Standing Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_Committee_of_the_National_People's_Congress) elected to meet between the annual NPC meetings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024218–220-168] NPC elections are [indirect](652583.md) and the CCP controls nominations.[^cite_note-Decoding_China-2021-169][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024218–220-168] The NPC elects the [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_China), [vice president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_China), [military chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Central_Military_Commission_\(China\)), and [chief justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_Supreme_People's_Court), and approves the president's nomination for [premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_China) (the head of government). In practice, the CCP leadership chooses candidates for these posts. The premier heads the [State Council](44498.md), which includes 26 ministers, such as the heads of ministries and commissions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024218–220-168] The [Chinese People's Political Consultative Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_People's_Political_Consultative_Conference) is an advisory body that formally leads the CCP's [united front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_front_\(China\)) system, which aims to gain support from [non-CCP intellectuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Party_intellectuals), eight [minor parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_parties_\(China\)), and [people's organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_organization).[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024232-171]

[China's current constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_China) dates to 1982, succeeding two earlier constitutions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang2026174,_180-172] Chinese governance is characterized by a high degree of political centralization but significant economic decentralization.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJin20237-173] Policy instruments and processes are often tested locally before being adopted more widely.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeilmann201814-174][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrussee202371-175] Surveys have generally shown that the Chinese public has a high level of support for the government.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJin2023136–137-176][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhai202387–89-178]

### Chinese Communist Party



[Xi Jinping](2017814.md)\
[CCP General Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) and [President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_China)



[Li Qiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Qiang)\
[Premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_China)



[Zhao Leji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhao_Leji)\
[Congress Chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Standing_Committee_of_the_National_People's_Congress)



[Wang Huning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Huning)\
[CPPCC Chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Chinese_People's_Political_Consultative_Conference)

The CCP is the founding and [sole ruling party](102459.md) of China,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024208-179] organized along [Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leninist) principles as a [vanguard party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanguardism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024b166-180] With about 98 million members, it is among the [world's largest political parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_political_parties), as well as the longest continuously ruling.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024208-179] The party's highest body is its [National Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Congress_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party), which mainly consists of members elected by party membership[^cite_note-182] and meets every five years. The National Congress elects the [Central Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Committee_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party), which convenes yearly as the highest party organ between congresses. The Central Committee ostensibly elects the party's top leadership, the [Politburo](51851.md), [Politburo Standing Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politburo_Standing_Committee_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) (PSC) and the [general secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party), its top leader. In practice, the committee typically approves a slate of candidates created by existing party leadership.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024211–212-183][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXiao20205-184]

The Politburo usually gathers once a month, while the smaller PSC is thought to meet weekly.[^cite_note-Asia-Society_2024-185] The current general secretary is [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), who took office on 15 November 2012. Xi concurrently serves as president and the chair of the [Central Military Commission](214345.md) (CMC); as such, he is referred to informally as the [paramount leader](262766.md) of China.[^cite_note-PhillipsGuardian2-186][^cite_note-187][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024597-3] The National Congress also elects the [Central Commission for Discipline Inspection](1526134.md), the party's main disciplinary and anti-corruption organ.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024215–216-188]

As the CCP is closely intertwined with the government, disputes within the party represent the main form of political contention in China.[^cite_note-Ma-2021-189][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024208,_211-190] The CCP controls government appointments, with most senior officials being CCP members.[^cite_note-Ma-2021-189] The appointments of [CCP cadres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadre_system_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) and the leadership of major [state-owned enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned_enterprises_of_China) and institutions are managed by the party's [Organization Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_Department_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024216-191] The Leninist principle of [democratic centralism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_centralism) mandates that party decisions are binding on all members.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang2026203–205-192]

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Administrative divisions of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_China), [Districts of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Hong_Kong), and [Municipalities and parishes of Macau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_and_parishes_of_Macau)

China is constitutionally a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) divided into 23 [provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_China),[^cite_note-TaiwanClaim-193] five [autonomous regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_regions_of_China) (each with a designated minority group), four [direct-administered municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct-administered_municipality), as well as the autonomous [special administrative regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_administrative_regions_of_China) (SARs) of Hong Kong and Macau.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024a9–11-194] Provinces and their equivalents are divided into [prefectures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefecture_\(China\)) and [prefecture-level cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefecture-level_city). These are divided into [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_\(China\)), [county-level cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County-level_city), and [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_\(China\)). Under these, [townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Township_\(China\)) are the basic form of local government. [Direct elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_China) occur only at the township and county levels and below.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang2026206–207-195]
