### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_China)



Diplomatic relations of China:

&#x20; People's Republic of China (PRC)

&#x20; Republic of China (ROC)

&#x20; States that have diplomatic relations with the PRC

&#x20; States that have diplomatic relations with the ROC

&#x20; States that have diplomatic relations with neither

China is one of five permanent members of the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) and the second largest contributor to the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2024150-144][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey2010286-196] China has the largest diplomatic network of any country: It has diplomatic relations with 179 [United Nations member states](31969.md) and [embassies in 174](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_China).[^cite_note-197] China is a member of the [G20](2206608.md), [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao2023100-198] [East Asia Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia_Summit),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao202369-199] [APEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation),[^cite_note-200] and other intergovernmental organizations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao2023100-198]

China is widely described as a [superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao2023204-201][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024a3-202] due to its influence in geopolitics, technology, manufacturing, economics and culture.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204] Although China moderated its relations with the West during the [reform era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_and_opening_up),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao20232-205] its rise in power has brought political tensions with many of its neighbors and [with the United States](277880.md), the current dominant superpower.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeng20221-206][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirk202310-207][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao2023223-208] Although China is among the United States' largest trade partners, their relationship is tempered by [a trade war](57055272.md)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2020168-209][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeng202246-210] and disagreements over the [political status of Taiwan](66555.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2020168-209]

China's foreign policy is based on the [Five Principles of Peaceful Coexistence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Principles_of_Peaceful_Coexistence), which include a principle of non-intervention in other countries' domestic affairs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERen20213–4-211] Per its policy of non-alignment, China has no [defense pacts](4991894.md) except for [its 1961 treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_Friendship,_Cooperation_and_Mutual_Assistance_between_China_and_the_DPRK) with [North Korea](21255.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao2023223-208] although it maintains close relationships with neighbors such as [Pakistan](23235.md)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao2023223-208] and Russia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeng20221-206] China has invested heavily in [developing countries](78449.md) across Asia, [Latin America](18524.md), and [Africa](5334607.md), creating a system of [development banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Development_bank) and loans through the [Belt and Road Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_and_Road_Initiative).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirk2023230-212]

### Military

Main article: [People's Liberation Army](66890.md)



[Chengdu J-20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chengdu_J-20) [5th generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth-generation_fighter) stealth fighter

The [People's Liberation Army](66890.md) (PLA) is the main armed forces of China, under the [direct control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Party_commands_the_gun) of the CCP.[^cite_note-213] It consists of four services: the [Ground Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Ground_Force), the [Navy](24918.md), the [Air Force](381445.md), and the [Rocket Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Rocket_Force), and four arms: the [Aerospace Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Aerospace_Force), the [Cyberspace Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Cyberspace_Force), the [Information Support Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Information_Support_Force), and the [Joint Logistics Support Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Liberation_Army_Joint_Logistics_Support_Force).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWirth20259-214] It has around 2 million active soldiers, and the world's [third largest stockpile of nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_China),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristensen_et_al.2025-215] and the world's largest navy by ship count.[^cite_note-216]

The PLA is considered one of the world's most powerful militaries and has [rapidly modernized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernization_of_the_People's_Liberation_Army) in the 21st century.[^cite_note-217] A 2026 study estimates that its 2025 expenditure was 2.27 trillion yuan (US\$336 billion), 12% of global military spending and 1.7% of China's GDP.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESIPRI20262-218] The PLA, the [People's Armed Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Armed_Police) and the [Militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militia_\(China\)) are commanded by the Central Military Commission. Due to the [chairman responsibility system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_responsibility_system), the [chairman of the CMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Central_Military_Commission_\(China\)), currently Xi Jinping, is the [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_command_of_the_armed_forces_in_China) of the PLA.[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024614,_632-220]

### Law and policing

Further information: [Judicial system of China](15169660.md), [Law of the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China), and [Law enforcement in China](265856.md)

A group of seven Chinese police officers in various uniforms

Police officers in Shanghai

The [Chinese judicial system](15169660.md) consists of [people's courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_people's_court) at provincial, regional, and local levels, alongside circuit courts for regional matters and specialized courts to handle professional disputes. The highest legal authority is the [Supreme People's Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_People's_Court).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang2026224,_238-221] The courts are closely controlled by the CCP, supervised by corresponding [people's congresses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/System_of_people's_congress), and led by CCP appointees.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang202628-222] [PRC law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) derives from a combination of [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)), [traditional Chinese law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_law), and [socialist law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_law).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang202682-223]

China's law enforcement system is managed by the [Ministry of Public Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Public_Security_\(China\)), which oversees [public security bureaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_security_bureau_\(China\)) (PSBs) in each province, themselves divided into local levels. Local sub-bureaus and stations handle most routine policing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEScoggins201890-224] The [People's Armed Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Armed_Police), a [paramilitary](146730.md) force, is administered by the CMC and supports emergency response measures.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWuthnow202275-225] The [social credit system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_credit_system) maintains a network of [blacklists](221697.md) to record businesses and individuals who violate regulations, court orders, or financial agreements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrussee202340,_44–46,_107-226] China has periodically mobilized anti-crime campaigns, using heavy penalties as a form of deterrence.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWangWang202487,_93–94-227] Violent crimes are the highest priority for enforcement, although they occur at relatively low rates.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhangZhangHong2026135-228] [Capital punishment](1461479.md) has declined since the Mao era, but remains common;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWangWang202487,_93–94-227] China has the most executions per year of any country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International20257-229]

### Human rights

Main article: [Human rights in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China)

&#x20;Various human rights organizations, United Nations assessments, and foreign governments have accused China of severe human rights violations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInboden20214–5-230][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPils2017Chapter_2,_'Institutional_Avenues_of_Human_Rights_Advocacy'-231] It has been accused of political repression, torture, [indefinite detention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indefinite_detention), restrictions on free speech and religious practice, excessive use of capital punishment, and the persecution of ethnic minorities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEdeLisle2024273-232]



China cracked down on political rights in Hong Kong following the [2019-2020 protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Hong_Kong_protests).

Although the Chinese constitution protects a wide variety of civil and political rights, these largely lack enforcement mechanisms. The [petitioning system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petitioning_\(China\)) allows citizens to raise complaints of wrongdoing, although the concerns are often left unaddressed, and some petitioners have faced punitive arrest or incarceration.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPils2017Chapter_2,_'Institutional_Avenues_of_Human_Rights_Advocacy'-231] In the 21st century, China has participated extensively in the [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInboden20214–5-230] but remains strongly critical of international scrutiny of its human rights, seeing it as interference in its internal affairs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInboden202115–18-233][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPils2017Chapter_2,_'Institutional_Avenues_of_Human_Rights_Advocacy'-231]

In [Xinjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang), China has persecuted the [Uyghur](31783.md) population through [widespread discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Uyghurs_in_China),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBovingdon2024523-234][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2026134-235] police crackdowns, and [restrictions on Islamic religious practice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobia_in_China) and the use of the [Uyghur language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uyghur_language). China interred over a million Uyghurs in a [network of internment camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang_internment_camps) in the late 2010s. These facilities have evolved into a system of prisons and [forced labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uyghur_forced_labour).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBovingdon2024542–544-236] Several UN [OHCHR](22463.md)[^cite_note-237] experts have equated it to "[enslavement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_China) as a [crime against humanity](61099.md)".[^cite_note-238] Alongside evidence of [compulsory sterilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_sterilization), many [genocide scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_scholar) have described these actions in Xinjiang as a [genocide](12441.md) or [cultural genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_genocide).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFinley2021355–359-239]

China has restricted the civil and political rights of [Hong Kong](13404.md) residents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2026134–135-240][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELo2024572–574-241] Since the [2019–2020 protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests), China has cracked down on the [pro-democracy movement](3291227.md) in the SAR and removed much of its political autonomy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELo2024572–574-241] China has cracked down on political expression in Tibet and strengthened surveillance since the late 2000s, restricting residents from obtaining passports and most foreigners from entering the region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarnett2024489–490,_512–513-242]

China does not recognize [same-sex marriage](92656.md) or [same-sex adoption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_adoption), and regularly censors [LGBTQ](78174325.md)-related media. Many Chinese hospitals provide [sexual conversion therapy](26410.md), viewing homosexuality as a mental disorder.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang_et_al.2019E440-243] [LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_China) advocates are surveilled and criminalized.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBernot2025167-244]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of China](19284336.md)

See also: [Income inequality in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_inequality_in_China) and [Industry in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_in_China)



The Shanghai Bund and [Lujiazui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lujiazui) seen from the [Shanghai Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Tower)

The modern PRC is often described by analysts as an example of [state capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_capitalism) or [party-state capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party-state_capitalism),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPearsonRithmireTsai202347–48-160] with both market forces and the state playing a major role in the economy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPearsonRithmireTsai2021207–213-245] Various state organs manage the economy according to the State Council's [five-year plans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five-year_plans_of_China).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang202369,_80–82-246] China describes its economic system as a [socialist market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_market_economy), in which the market plays a decisive role in resource allocation under the control of a [socialist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_state_\(communism\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEde_Rambures20159–11-247][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDing2009235–241-248] The Chinese economy has expanded more than 20 times since 2000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang20239-249] It is now the [second largest economy by GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) (135 trillion yuan)[^cite_note-250] and [the largest when adjusted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)) for [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity).[^cite_note-251] China has brought roughly 800 million people out of extreme [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_China), more than any other country in history.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang20233–4-252][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELo202511-253]

A color photograph of various men and women standing in a dark factory, with glowing red steel being poured

Workers at a steel foundry in [Shanghai](27643.md), 1978

China is the world's leading manufacturing power, accounting for 28% of global manufacturing as of 2024;[^cite_note-254] it is the largest producer of [steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel_industry_in_China),[^cite_note-255] [rare earths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare-earth_industry_in_China),[^cite_note-256] ships,[^cite_note-257] and automobiles,[^cite_note-258] as well as the largest exporter of electronics[^cite_note-259] and [textiles](51498947.md).[^cite_note-260]

The private sector constitutes the largest portion of the Chinese economy, with about 30–40% under state ownership. State-owned companies make less than 10% of exports, but are predominant in industries such as transportation, utilities, and financial services.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang20237-261] Since the 1990s, China has decentralized its economic planning, with about 85% of government spending delegated to the provincial or local level,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELo202519-262] and local governments adapting national economic plans to suit local interests.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang202369,_80–82-246]

China's main [stock exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_exchanges) are [Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Stock_Exchange) and [Shenzhen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenzhen_Stock_Exchange). Its stock market is the second largest, behind the US.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeWei2023456-263] The [Chinese banking system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_China) makes up a much larger share of the economy than most other countries. Four state-owned banks hold about 40% of the sector's assets as of 2022.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeWei2023464–467-264] China has struggled to establish its currency, the [renminbi](66958.md) (in units of yuan), as an international [reserve currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_currency);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang2023179-265] as of 2025, it is the seventh largest currency in foreign exchange reserves.[^cite_note-266]

About a third of China's workforce consists of domestic migrant workers, the [floating population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floating_population). The *[hukou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hukou)* system restricts citizens' access to services and utilities outside of their registered home, leading to pronounced inequalities between urban and rural workers. Especially in larger cities, only those with local *hukou* may be able to buy property, though *hukou* restrictions have loosened significantly in the 21st century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang2023110–113-267][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELo202513-268][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJin202323-269]

According to 2026 state data, China's urban unemployment rate was 5.0%.[^cite_note-270] China has a high [debt-to-GDP ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debt-to-GDP_ratio) (almost 300% in 2020),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang20238-271] with [household debt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Household_debt) at about 65% of the GDP.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang2023166-272] China has faced a [property sector crisis](68779824.md) since 2020. By the late 2010s, Chinese real estate assets far exceed the combined total of stocks, [bonds](60737.md), and [deposits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deposit_\(finance\)).[^cite_note-273]

### Science and technology

Main articles: [Science and technology in China](277914.md), [List of Chinese discoveries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chinese_discoveries), and [List of Chinese inventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chinese_inventions)



Launch of [Shenzhou 13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenzhou_13) by a [Long March 2F](340934.md) rocket

As the world's largest economy for much of its history, many inventions and scientific discoveries have been produced in China, including paper, printing, the compass, and gunpowder (referred to as the [Four Great Inventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Great_Inventions)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012467-274] China has pursued rapid technological development since the reform era.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirk2023109-275] It has made great advances in the fields of [artificial intelligence](1164.md), [robotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robotics), and [biotechnology](4502.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirk20236-276] while the Xi administration has prioritized [green technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_technology)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChan2024150-144] and reducing Chinese dependence on foreign technology.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZweig2024311–312-277] It has emphasized [technocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technocracy) leadership in the 2020s, promoting experts in scientific fields to national leadership positions,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024226-278] while bringing major private technology firms such as [Tencent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tencent) and [Alibaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alibaba_Group) under state regulation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZweig2024308-279] Geopolitical tensions have led Europe and the United States to restrict Chinese access to their technology,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirk20237–8-280] especially [semiconductors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semiconductor),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHamdaniBelfencha20241–2-281] and launch sanctions targeting the telecommunications and electronics firm [Huawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huawei).[^cite_note-282]

Since launching [its first satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dong_Fang_Hong_1) in 1970,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarvey201949–50-283] China has maintained [an active space program](343674.md). After the United States and the Soviet Union, China became the third to independently put astronauts into orbit, with a [2003 mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenzhou_5).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarvey2019x–xi-284] The [Chinese Lunar Exploration Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Lunar_Exploration_Program) has sent multiple robotic landers to the Moon,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarvey2019443-285] the [Planetary Exploration of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planetary_Exploration_of_China) program has sent [a lander and rover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tianwen-1) to [Mars](14640471.md),[^cite_note-286] and the [Tiangong space station](2481401.md) has hosted crewed operations in [Low Earth orbit](47568.md) since 2021.[^cite_note-287]

### Infrastructure and transportation

Main articles: [Transport in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_China) and [Telecommunications in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_China)

A train running on an elevated track through a commercial area

A [Fuxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuxing_\(train\)) high-speed train running in Beijing

Since the 1990s, automobiles have become a common form of transport in China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChenPanShenWang20201–3-288] According to state data, the Chinese highway network exceeded 6,000,000 km (3,700,000 mi) by the end of 2021, having grown 30% over the past decade. In addition to local and provincial routes, this network includes the [national highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_highways_of_China), which are divided between the ordinary highways (of various classes) and the [national expressway system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_China). China has the largest highway and rail systems in the world by capacity,[^cite_note-National_Development_and_Reform_Commission-2022-289][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFu202037–39-290] along with the [second longest rail network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_rail_transport_network_size) at roughly 165,000 km (102,526 mi).[^cite_note-Yang_2025-291] According to 2022 state data, over 95% of cities exceeding one million residents have access to [high-speed rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail_in_China).[^cite_note-National_Development_and_Reform_Commission-2022-289] Out of the [50 busiest container ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_container_ports), 18 are located in China, including the world's busiest, the [Port of Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Shanghai).[^cite_note-292] China has the largest domestic aviation market by passenger capacity, and maintains 262 airports as of 2025.[^cite_note-293]

Chinese cities are disparate in their level of infrastructure. [Tier-one cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_city_tier_system) such as Beijing and Shanghai have elaborate and advanced infrastructure networks, while smaller cities and rural areas often have outdated and underfunded transit and public utilities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang20234–5-294] Water resources in China are unevenly distributed. With industrialization and rapid development, the drier north has faced endemic water shortages. To combat this, the [South–North Water Transfer Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South%E2%80%93North_Water_Transfer_Project) pumps water from the Yangtze basin to supply northern cities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWebberCrow-MillerRogers2017370–375-295]

A wide shot of the Three Gorges Dam

The [Three Gorges Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Gorges_Dam), the largest hydroelectric dam in the world

The [State Grid Corporation of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Grid_Corporation_of_China), the world's largest [utility company](171136.md), manages the [electrical grid](20344155.md) for most of the country, with the [China Southern Power Grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Southern_Power_Grid) as the electric utility in some southern provinces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXu2019222–223-296] Power generation is primarily managed by five state-owned corporations, alongside local and provincial utilities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXu2019222–223-296][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWu201986-297] Most of China's renewable energy comes from [hydropower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectricity_in_China).[^cite_note-298] The [Three Gorges Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Gorges_Dam) is the [world's largest power station by capacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_power_stations).[^cite_note-299]

China has the largest number of internet users and websites of any country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStockmannLuo2026238-300] According to state data, it has 1.125 billion internet users as of 2025, about 80% of its population.[^cite_note-301] It has an extensive [system of internet censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_China). The [Great Firewall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Firewall), a system of internet controls and regulations, blocks access to many foreign websites. Users can circumvent it through [virtual private networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_private_network), but these face occasional crackdowns.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts20182–4-302] The [Cyberspace Administration of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyberspace_Administration_of_China) is the national internet regulator and censor.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts2018106–107-303][^cite_note-Horsley_2022-304] Most [telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_China) are operated by state-owned companies.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWu201986-297]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of China](23238.md)

See also: [Chinese nationality law](4600725.md) and [Family planning policies of China](37816243.md)



Map of China's population density across third-level administrative divisions. Based on the results of the 2020 census.

The [2020 Chinese census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Chinese_census) recorded the population as 1,411,778,724. About 17.95% were 14 years old or younger, 63.35% were between 15 and 59, and 18.7% were over 60.[^cite_note-2020_census2-305] Between 2010 and 2020, the average population growth rate was 0.53%.[^cite_note-2020_census2-305] Since 2022, deaths have outpaced births.[^cite_note-Wu-2026-306] China has one of the lowest [total fertility rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) (births per woman) in the world, around 1.0 as of 2025.[^cite_note-307] [China has a rapidly aging population](60912277.md): the proportion of Chinese over 65 is predicted to reach 26% by 2050, which would create a large economic and social burden from increased need for caretakers and medical assistance.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHan_et_al.202011–13-308]

The [National Bureau of Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bureau_of_Statistics_of_China) estimated that the population fell 850,000 from 2021 to 2022, the first decline since 1961.[^cite_note-309] In 2025, China recorded 5.6 births per 1000 people, the lowest recorded birthrate since at least 1949.[^cite_note-Wu-2026-306] China's population is concentrated in the eastern half of the country; although 64% of China's territory is west of the [Heihe–Tengchong Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heihe%E2%80%93Tengchong_Line), as of 2020 only 6.5% of its population lives west of the line.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXiao202617-310]

Spurred by fears of [overpopulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overpopulation), China maintained a [one-child policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-child_policy) from 1979 to 2015, restricting most families to a single child. The policy was most strictly enforced in urban areas, while families from ethnic minorities and rural communities were often exempt from one-child limits. A two-child policy was introduced in 2015,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZengHesketh20161930–1933-311] and in July 2021, family size limits as well as penalties for exceeding them were removed.[^cite_note-312] The policy resulted in a skewed [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_ratio), as many families preferred to have sons, who were able to continue the patrilineal line and were perceived as better able to support the family. Through a combination of infanticide, [sex-selective abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex-selective_abortion), and unreported female births, there were 120 boys born for every 100 girls by the late 1990s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZengHesketh20161930–1933-311][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChenZhang20191057–1059-313]

China maintains a restrictive [immigration policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_China), with permanent residence granted to only around 12,000 foreigners as of 2023,[^cite_note-314] while an additional 845,697 [foreign nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_national) lived in mainland China as of the 2020 census.[^cite_note-315] The [Chinese diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_diaspora) is among the largest on earth, comprising about 40 million people, mostly descended from [emigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_emigration) during the 19th and early 20th centuries. About two-thirds of this diaspora reside in [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Indonesians), [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_Chinese), [Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysian_Chinese), and the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Americans).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPostonWong2016351,_362,_368-316]

### Cities and urbanization

Main article: [Urbanization in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanization_in_China)

See also: [List of cities in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_China), [List of cities in China by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_China_by_population), and [Megalopolises in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalopolises_in_China)



[Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou), a major transportation hub and one of China's three largest cities

China [has urbanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanization_in_China) significantly since the 1980s. The percentage of the country's population living in urban areas increased from 20% in 1980 to over 68% in 2025, according to Chinese government figures,[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] while the United Nations calculates the 2025 urbanization rate as 83.7%.[^cite_note-The-Economist_2026-319] Cities in China often include smaller towns and rural areas under their jurisdiction.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQiDeng20246-320] [Shanghai](27643.md) is its [most populous city by urban area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_China_by_population).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQiDeng202411-321] Two [urban agglomerations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_agglomerations), the [Pearl Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_River_Delta) and [Yangtze Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangtze_Delta), contain many of the largest cities and tens of millions of people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQiDeng20248–10-322]

The figures below are from the 2020 census and are only estimates of the urban populations within administrative city limits; a different ranking exists for total municipal populations. The large floating population of migrant workers make conducting censuses in urban areas difficult.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQiDeng20246-320]

### Languages and ethnic groups

Main articles: [Languages of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_China) and [List of ethnic groups in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ethnic_groups_in_China)

A demographic map of China shaded by region to indicate different language families and minority groups.

An ethnolinguistic map of China (1967), illustrating the geographic distribution of [language families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_family) and ethnic groups

China legally recognizes [56 distinct ethnic groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ethnic_groups_in_China). The largest of these are the [Han Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese), who constitute more than 91% of the total population. The minority groups are generally found in the western and border regions of China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZang20161–3-328] Social and wealth inequalities exist between many minority groups and the Han, especially in the rural [southwestern areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwestern_China).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZang20168,_17-329] The [Zhuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuang_people) of the southwest are the most populous minority group;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoseph2024a8-330] other major groups include the [Uyghurs](31783.md), [Hui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hui_people), [Mongols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongols_in_China), and [Tibetans](249458.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZang201610–13-331]

Most Han are speakers of [Mandarin Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese). [Standard Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Chinese), largely based off the [Beijing dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_dialect), is the official language of China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBradley2007354–355,_385-332] Around 300 million people speak other [Chinese languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinitic_languages) (often described as 方言—local varieties or [dialects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dialect)—in Chinese sources). While also written with [Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_characters), these are not highly [mutually intelligible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_intelligibility) with Mandarin or each other. Major branches of Chinese include Mandarin, [Wu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wu_Chinese), [Yue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yue_Chinese) (Cantonese), [Min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Min_Chinese), [Gan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gan_Chinese), [Hakka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakka_Chinese), and [Xiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiang_Chinese); the latter six are mainly spoken in Southern China. Since the 1950s, local dialects have steadily assimilated into Mandarin or one of the main regional registers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBradley2007354–355-333]

[Simplified Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters), first standardized in the 1950s, are used to write Chinese throughout mainland China, while [traditional Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_characters) remain in use in the [SARs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_administrative_regions_of_China) and Taiwan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYangWang20181134-334] [Hanyu Pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin) is the official [romanization system for Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Chinese) in the PRC.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChen2016484-335]

Other language families are found throughout China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBradley2007351–354-336] Distantly related to Chinese, [Tibeto-Burman languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibeto-Burman_languages) are found in the west and southwest.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBradley2007355-337] In southwestern China, many [Hmong–Mien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hmong%E2%80%93Mien_languages) and [Kra–Dai languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kra%E2%80%93Dai_languages) are spoken by minority groups. Offshoots of the [Austroasiatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austroasiatic_languages) and [Austronesian language families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_languages) are also found in the south in smaller numbers. In the northwest, [Turkic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkic_languages) (including [Uyghur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uyghur_language)) and [Mongolic languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongolic_languages) are found, as well as two [Indo-European languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_languages) ([Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language) and [Tajik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tajik_language)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBradley2007351–354-336] Several [Tungusic languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tungusic_languages) are found in northwestern and northeastern China.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBradley2007380-338] Since the 2010s, the Chinese government has intensified [linguistic assimilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linguistic_assimilation) policies, generally requiring schools to use Standard Chinese.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarnett2024514-339]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_China)

See also: [Irreligion in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_China) and [Antireligious campaigns in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antireligious_campaigns_in_China)

A Chinese painting showing a scene of the Buddha, Laozi, and Confucius standing together and smiling by a bridge and a tree

Ming dynasty painting depicting the sages of the [three teachings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_teachings): [the Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Buddha), [Laozi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laozi), and [Confucius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucius)

The Chinese religious tradition is heavily syncretic, incorporating various [folk religious practices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_folk_religion) alongside [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism), [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism), and [Buddhism](3267529.md). These '[three teachings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_teachings)' were the three main institutionalized schools of Chinese religion. These exist alongside introduced minority religions such as [Christianity](5211.md) and [Islam](6037917.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012378-340][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYaoZhao20106–8,_13–14-341] as well as [various new religious movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_salvationist_religions).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYaoZhao20109-342] Statistics on affiliation are difficult to gather due to varying definitions of religion and the diffusive nature of local traditions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYaoZhao20109–11-343] Many people follow religious teachings and practices without considering themselves adherents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYaoZhao20109–11-343] [Local deities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tudigong) are commonly worshiped alongside widely known gods, such as [Guan Yu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guan_Yu) and [Mazu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazu).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETam201141–43-344]

Confucianism is a philosophical and spiritual tradition that originated in the Eastern Zhou, emphasizing the study of the [classics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_classics) and the relations of the individual, society, and [heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tian).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBillioud202075-345][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESwain201724–32-346] It has sometimes been identified as a religion, following Western influences.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBillioud202075-345] Daoism emphasizes the *[Dao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dao)* ('way') as the world's origin and fundamental force, and seeks to harness it through spiritual cultivation. Initially a philosophical movement during the Eastern Zhou, the religious tradition of Daoism emerged during the 2nd century CE.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012380–382-347][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWei201513–15,_20-348] [Mahayana Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana) was introduced to China during the Han dynasty and spread rapidly over the following centuries. Although often dismissed by Confucian elites, Buddhism greatly influenced other Chinese religious and philosophical traditions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012384-349]

Although China and CCP are legally [atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_atheism), [freedom of religion](2203791.md) is formally guaranteed by the Chinese constitution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYaoZhao20109–11-343] China [officially recognizes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laws_regarding_religious_activities_in_China) five religions: [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_China), Taoism, [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_China_\(1912%E2%80%93present\)), [Catholicism](1714754.md), and [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_China). Formal religious practice is managed by "patriotic associations" for each of the recognized faiths overseen by the CCP's [United Front Work Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Front_Work_Department).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhu202096–97-350] However, the majority of religious activities take place outside of registered organizations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFeuchtwang20204–6-351][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMadsen202027–28-352] China has attempted to crack down on some forms of unregistered practice, such as Protestant [house churches](357470.md). Religious practices among minority groups, such as Islam among the Uyghur and [Tibetan Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibetan_Buddhism), have come under state scrutiny and regulation over fears of [separatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secession_in_China).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMadsen202027–28-352]

### Education

Main articles: [Education in China](407843.md) and [Higher education in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education_in_China)

See also: [Literacy in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_in_China)

A photo of a group of students in a Chinese junior high school classroom, seated at desks

Students at a junior high school classroom in [Zhejiang](96602.md), 2019

Following an optional two to three years of [preschool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preschool_in_China?action=edit\&redlink=1), China has nine years of free [compulsory education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education), generally with six years of elementary school and three years of junior high school.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiaFeng20257-353] If they pass entrance examinations, students may then attend a senior high school, choosing between an academic or [vocational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_school) track.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiaFeng20259–10-354] The *[gaokao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaokao)* exams allow access to [undergraduate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undergraduate_education) higher education, with additional examinations required to attend postgraduate programs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiaFeng20259–10-354] As of 2022, 95% of junior high students continue to high school, while higher education has a 59.6% enrollment rate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiaFeng202517-355]

As of 2022, China had 158 million students enrolled in compulsory school, and 46.5 million enrolled in higher education. There are over 200,000 elementary and junior high schools, over 22,000 secondary schools, and over 3,000 colleges and universities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiaFeng202516–18-356] Both private and public schools exist at all levels, with 10.6% of elementary and junior high students attending private schools.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJiaFeng20257,_10-357] China became the world's [largest publisher of scientific papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_scientific_and_technical_journal_articles) in 2016.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETollefson2018390-358] [Foreign language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_education) programs are mandatory from 3rd grade. Generally focused on English, other languages are increasingly being taught, especially at the senior high level.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang20243–4-359]

### Health

Main article: [Health in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_China)

See also: [Medicine in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicine_in_China) and [Pharmaceutical industry in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceutical_industry_in_China)

A large pharmacy in China, with western prepackaged medicines at the left, and various herbal medicines in cabinets at the right

A pharmacy in [Guangxi](47836950.md), selling both Western and [traditional Chinese medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_medicine)

The vast majority of [primary health care institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_health_care) in China offer both [allopathic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allopathic_medicine) and [traditional Chinese medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_medicine) (TCM). In 2017, there were approximately 9,000 community health centers in urban areas—which oversaw roughly 25,000 health stations—and 37,000 township health centers in rural areas. There were about 639,000 village clinics, which operate as independent, for-profit enterprises subsidized by the state.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELi_et_al.20172586-360][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWangWang20198-361]

The [National Health Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Health_Commission), together with its local counterparts, oversees health policy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESunCondéWang2026417-362] After the beginning of reform and opening-up in 1978, Chinese public health improved rapidly from better nutrition, although many free health services provided in the countryside disappeared. [Healthcare in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_China) became mostly privatized and experienced a significant quality increase.[^cite_note-363] China is a major producer and exporter of [active pharmaceutical ingredients](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_ingredient).[^cite_note-364]

According to 2025 state data, the [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) at birth exceeds 79 years,[^cite_note-365] and the [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) is 4 per 1,000.[^cite_note-366] Rates of [stunted growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stunted_growth) declined from 33.1% in 1990 to 4.5% in 2024.[^cite_note-367] Mental health services are underfunded and unevenly distributed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQueLuShi20191-368] The [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md) was first identified in [Wuhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuhan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENovel_Coronavirus_Pneumonia_Emergency_Response_Epidemiology_Team2020145-369] China maintained a [zero-COVID policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero-COVID), using [lockdowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_lockdowns), [quarantines](25237.md), and [contact tracing](1724851.md) to curb its transmission, until [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_COVID-19_protests_in_China) in November–December 2022.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBa_et_al.20231–2-370]

## Culture

Main articles: [Chinese culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_culture) and [Culture of the People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China)

A group of people walking through an avenue over which is hung many dozens of red paper lanterns and other festive ornaments

Red [paper lanterns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paper_lantern) hung up for [Chinese New Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_New_Year) festivities in Beijing

Beginning in the Qin and Han dynasties and continuing over the following millennia, [an area of Chinese cultural influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinosphere) grew to encompass much of East Asia. Japan, Korea, and Vietnam adopted various political, philosophical, religious, and aesthetic concepts from China, facilitated by their adoption of [Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_characters) and participation in the [tributary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tributary_system_of_China).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2020xxii–xxiv-371] [China has 60 World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_China), including the [Great Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China) and Grand Canal, the second most of any country (behind [Italy](14532.md)).[^cite_note-372]

Chinese culture has historically emphasized family connections, with [filial piety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filial_piety) and the [veneration of ancestors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veneration_of_the_dead) seen as vital duties under Confucian ethics. Prior to the 20th century, family relations were highly [patriarchal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarchy), and [arranged marriages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arranged_marriage) were commonplace.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMortonLewis2005272–274-373] The CCP pushed for gender equality after coming to power, establishing the [All-China Women's Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-China_Women's_Federation) to manage women's issues. However, many patriarchal gender norms remain prevalent, and women face lower employment rates and [occupational gender segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_gender_segregation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECheungTang201719–20,_23–25-374]

Prior to the adoption of the [Gregorian calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorian_calendar) in 1912, China traditionally used a 12-month [lunisolar calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunisolar_calendar).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012498,_501-375] The 60-part [sexagenary cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexagenary_cycle) was often used to enumerate days as well as years,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012496–497-376] alongside the twelve-year [Chinese zodiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_zodiac).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012518-377] [Major holidays and festivals in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_China) include the [Chinese New Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_New_Year) (or Spring Festival), the [Qingming Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qingming_Festival), the [Mid-Autumn Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Autumn_Festival), and [National Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Day_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper201137–39-378]

### Art

Main article: [Chinese art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_art)

A hand fan with a flower motif and a poem painted on its surface

An 18th-century [hand fan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_fan) with a poem and nature painting

Traditional Chinese art makes use of a wide array of common motifs, metaphors, and symbols, often using nature as allegory.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011153–154-379] During the imperial era, skill in art was a marker of refinement and high social status.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011152–153-380] By the Song period, [landscape paintings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landscape_painting) became among the most prestigious genres.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClunas200953–55-381] Renowned art pieces often bear inscriptions and [seals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seals_in_the_Sinosphere) from their previous owners, allowing for the tracing of [provenance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provenance) over long periods of time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClunas2009139-382]

Chinese paintings are traditionally made with [ink brushes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ink_brush) on paper or [silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011152–153-380] Horizontal [handscrolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handscroll) and vertical [hanging scrolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanging_scroll) are common formats, alongside [wall paintings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural), [folding screens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folding_screen) and [hand fans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_fan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClunas200948–50-383] [Calligraphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_calligraphy), emerging during the 3rd and 4th centuries CE, was traditionally regarded in China as the most prestigious form of art, and remains among the most widely practiced.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011152-384][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClunas2009135–137-385] Western painting styles gained popularity during the early 20th century,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndrewsShen201274-386] while the Mao government promoted art as a means to embody socialist ideals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClunas2009212–219-387] The 21st century has seen a boom in the international influence of Chinese [contemporary art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_art).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndrewsShen2012291–296-388]

Pottery and metal crafts in China date to ancient times.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClunas200915–18-389] [Porcelain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porcelain), also known as china, became a common form of ceramic during the 7th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011167-390] By the 14th century, it became popular to decorate porcelain vessels with elaborate [blue and white patterns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_and_white_pottery), which became the most popular form of Chinese ceramics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011174–175-391] [Jade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jade) crafts and [lacquerware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lacquerware) have been common decorative mediums since antiquity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011226,_230-392]

#### Architecture

Further information: [Chinese architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_architecture)

An aerial view of the Forbidden City centered on the Gate of Divine Prowess

The [Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City) in Beijing, with the [Gate of Divine Prowess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gate_of_Divine_Prowess) at center

Wood is the most commonly used material in traditional [Chinese architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_architecture). Roofs of important buildings, generally ceramic tile, are often ornate and [hipped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip_roof).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhardt20191–2-393] Traditional buildings are often arranged around [cardinal directions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_direction), with grandeur expressed through large complexes, often featuring [courtyards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courtyard) and large gates.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhardt20195–6-394] The spiritual concept of [feng shui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feng_shui) influences the layout of houses and settlements, oriented around the flow of *[qi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qi)*, vital energy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETam201134–35-395]

[Pagodas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagoda), multi-story religious towers far taller than other forms of traditional architecture, were introduced to China alongside Buddhism, and comprise many of China's oldest buildings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhardt20196,_95-396] Western-style architecture was introduced during the 1800s,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhardt2019319–321-397] while domestic architects began to implement Western design principles in the 1900s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhardt2019321–324-398] The reform era has seen a boom in [contemporary](3970948.md) skyscrapers and offices, which have largely supplanted traditional architecture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011287–289-399]

#### Performing arts

Further information: [Music of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_China)

Two Peking Opera actors performing on stage in ornate costumes

Two actors in a performance of [*Farewell My Concubine*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hegemon-King_Bids_His_Lady_Farewell), a [Peking opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_opera)

The [music of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_China) is highly diverse and regional.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELau200827–28-400] Traditional Chinese music uses a wide variety of instruments, including the *[dizi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dizi_\(instrument\))* (a flute), *[erhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erhu)* (a [bowed string instrument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowed_string_instrument)), *[guqin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guqin)* (a plucked string instrument), *[guzheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guzheng)* (a plucked [zither](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zither)), *[pipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pipa)* (a [lute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lute)), *[sheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheng_\(instrument\))* (a [mouth organ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouth_organ)), *[suona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suona)* (a wind instrument), and various types of bells and drums.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011239–241-401][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELau200838,_51-402] Western-style music spread during the early 20th century, leading the [Chinese orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_orchestra) to develop as a fusion of Western orchestral music with Chinese instruments. Western [classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_music) remains highly prestigious.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELau200834–39,_91-403] Inspired by Western [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) and boosted by the introduction of recording studios, [Mandarin pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_pop_music) emerged in Shanghai in the 1920s, while the [folk rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_rock)-inspired [Cantopop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantopop) became popular around Hong Kong during the 1970s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELau2008105–108-404] After reform and opening-up, various forms of pop and rock music reached widespread popularity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELau2008109–111-405]

[Chinese opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_opera) emerged from *[shuochang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shuochang)*, folk narrative singing traditions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillerChurch2015127–128-406] Influenced by various regional traditions and *[kunqu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunqu)*, a genre popular during the Ming dynasty, [Peking opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_opera) reached national popularity during the Qing dynasty,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillerChurch2015130–131-407] while various local and regional forms of opera emerged across the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMillerChurch2015127–128-406] Maoist China promoted [revolutionary opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_opera) and communist-oriented folk music.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuiper2011254–256-408]

By the Han period, [Chinese dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_in_China) was a frequent subject of art and poetry, as well as an element of religious rites.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChangFrederiksen201612–15,_19-409] Dancers often performed for the imperial courts,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChangFrederiksen201619–20-410] while Chinese opera dances led to increasing numbers of dance troupes across all levels of society during the Qing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChangFrederiksen201627–29-411] Western dances gained popularity in the early 20th century, while the CCP promoted [folk dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_dance) such as *[yangge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangge)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChangFrederiksen201634–35,_46–47-412]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Chinese cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_cuisine)

A man in a restaurant kitchen stands at a food stand with three hot wok slots, two of which are occupied by woks

A man at a [Chinese Muslim restaurant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Islamic_cuisine) in [Xi'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi'an) preparing food in [woks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wok)

Chinese meals generally incorporate a grain (*fan*) alongside meat or vegetable-based dishes (*cai*). Flour—as [steamed buns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantou), [dumplings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dumpling), [pancakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bing_\(bread\)), or [noodles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_noodles)—is the primary grain in the north, alongside [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize), [millet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millet), [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice), and in some regions, [sorghum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorghum). Rice—steamed or in the form of [congee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congee), [rice noodles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice_noodles) or [rice cakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice_cake)—dominates in the south. Although Chinese cuisine is highly diverse and regional, pork, chicken, fish, and duck are the most common meats.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChang19777–8-413][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu200410–13-414][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHsuHsu1977300-415] Soybeans are a common staple nationally made into products such as [tofu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tofu) and [soy sauce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soy_sauce).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu200412–13-416] [Stir frying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stir_frying) in [woks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wok) is the most common cooking method, alongside deep-frying, steaming,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu2004119–123-417] and [hot pot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_pot), where dishes are cooked in simmering broth at the table.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndersonAnderson1977358-418]

People in China generally eat three meals a day,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHsuHsu1977304-419][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu200433-420] often with a simple breakfast and a more elaborate dinner.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu200440-421] [Chopsticks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chopsticks) are the main [eating utensil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eating_utensil).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu200427–29-422] Dishes are usually served to be shared among multiple people, rather than served in individual portions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiu200433-420] Hot tea almost always accompanies meals across the country, in lieu of water. Alcohol such as [rice wines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice_wine) and the distilled liquor [baijiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baijiu) are commonly served with meals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHsuHsu1977307–308-423] Chinese cuisine was traditionally divided into four great traditions: [Lu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shandong_cuisine) (Shandong), [Yang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huaiyang_cuisine) (Jiangsu), [Chuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sichuan_cuisine) (Sichuan), and [Yue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese_cuisine) (Guangdong).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012456-424][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndersonAnderson1977353–354-425] Chinese Buddhists have developed a [unique cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_cuisine) that [avoids meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetarianism_in_China), while [Chinese Islamic cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Islamic_cuisine) excludes pork and alcohol.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012441-426][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaiFischer2016162–164-427]

### Fashion

Main article: [Chinese clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_clothing)

A Chinese painting of a group of men and women reveling at a banquet

Traditional clothing in a [Song dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_dynasty) painting&#x20;

Various forms of [robe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robe) served as the main form of clothing for men and women throughout Chinese history, adapting over the centuries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012182–183-428] Silk, hemp, and later cotton were the main textiles.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012180–181-429] Both men and women generally bound their hair with caps or pins, except during periods where foreign hairstyles were enforced by the elite.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012182–183-428]

Chinese fashions changed greatly during the 20th century, as traditional designs largely gave way to Western clothing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFinnane20081–3-430] The *[qipao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qipao)* became a popular form of women's dress during the early 20th century, but are today rare outside of formal occasions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFinnane2008297,_300–301-431] After a focus on simple, practical clothing during the Mao era,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFinnane2008257-432][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012184-433] the reform era saw the emergence of a Western-style fashion industry.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFinnane2008263–265-434] In the 21st century, the [Hanfu movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanfu_Movement) has sought to promote a reconstructed form of traditional Chinese clothing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFanIp2023786–787-435]

### Literature

Main articles: [Chinese literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_literature) and [Chinese poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_poetry)

A group of bamboo slips with old Chinese characters written on them

[Bamboo slips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamboo_and_wooden_slips) from the [Warring States period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warring_States_period), containing part of a commentary on the *[Classic of Poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_of_Poetry)*

China has among the longest continuous literary traditions in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOwen2011xx-436] Late Shang-era [oracle bone inscriptions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle_bone_script) are the oldest known form of Chinese writing,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199712-42] while the oldest surviving literary works—such as the *[Book of Documents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Documents)—*&#x64;ate to the early Zhou.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEbrey201030-437] The Chinese literary canon was historically centered on the [Five Classics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Classics) (ancient transmitted texts) and the [Four Books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Books_and_Five_Classics) (central Confucian texts).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199753–54-438] [Writings on history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_historiography) were ubiquitous and closely connected to literature since ancient times, seen as a way to guide later generations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012599,_603–604-439]

The literati regarded literature as primarily [didactic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didacticism), with fiction seen as useless and potentially corruptive.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft19979–10-440][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199756–57-441] However, poetry was viewed highly for its ability to convey the nature of reality. The [poetry of the Tang era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tang_poetry) was particularly prized in later dynasties.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199752–53-442][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012401–402-443] Before the early 20th century, fiction was generally written in [vernacular Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Written_vernacular_Chinese), with formal works generally in [Classical Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Chinese).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIdemaHaft199758-444] Popular vernacular literature spread with the introduction of printing, resulting in works such as the [Four Classic Novels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_Chinese_Novels) of the Ming and Qing periods.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012411-445]

Modern Chinese literature has been heavily influenced by the political upheavals of the 19th and 20th centuries, seeking to explain the political climate or imagine its future.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang2017245–247-446] [New Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Culture_Movement) writers such as [Lu Xun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lu_Xun) promoted the importance of fiction and [vernacular Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Written_vernacular_Chinese), experimenting with new modernist forms,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang2017237–239-447] while writers of the Reform era sought to explain the trauma of the Mao era.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWang2017243–245-448] [Chinese online literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_online_literature) has become popular alongside the growth of the internet, with about half of Chinese internet users regularly consuming it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHan202621–22-449]

### Media

Main article: [Mass media in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_China)

China's largest media company is [China Central Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Central_Television), which competes with various provincial television networks. China is the largest online video market, with its major streaming platforms including [iQIYI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IQIYI), [Youku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youku), and [Sohu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sohu).[^cite_note-BBC_2023-450] As of 2025, the [Chinese film industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_China) is the second largest by global box office earnings (behind the United States), although 98% of earnings are domestic. China limits the number of foreign films that can be shown domestically. According to state sources, it has over 90,000 cinema screens as of 2025, more than any other country.[^cite_note-451]

As of 2023, China has over 2,600 radio stations, all state-owned, and over 1,900 newspapers. Cities generally have a newspaper published by the local government and another by the local CCP branch. Journalism is tightly controlled and monitored by the CCP, with content critical of the government censored. Foreign Chinese-language news outlets are blocked, while English-language outlets are occasionally available but often censored. Prominent newspapers in China include the *[People's Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Daily),* *[Global Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Times)*, *[China Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Daily)*, and *[Reference News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reference_News).*[^cite_note-BBC_2023-450] The most popular social media platform is [WeChat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WeChat). Other major platforms include the [microblogging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microblogging) service [Weibo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weibo), the news and content aggregator [Toutiao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toutiao), the video-sharing platform [Xigua Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xigua_Video), and the short-form video platform [Douyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douyin), available internationally as [TikTok](56822861.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang202267-452]

### Sport and games

Main article: [Sport in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_China)

Further information: [Video games in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_games_in_China)

A group of people playing a game of mahjong

[Mahjong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahjong) originated as a gambling game during the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty).

Traditional sports such as [martial arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_arts) and [dragon boat racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_boat) have been practiced in China for over a millennium.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZheng_et_al.2018469-453] The ball game *[cuju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuju)* predates the Qin dynasty and remained popular for millennia, declining during the late [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty). [Chuiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuiwan) (similar to modern [golf](19568112.md)) and [polo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polo) were popular sports among elites throughout portions of the imperial period, while commoners played regional ball games.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiuQu202351-454][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuangWang202317-455] Evolving out of Chinese martial arts in the Qing period, [tai chi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tai_chi) incorporates Taoist practices into a form of [gymnastics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymnastics).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVercammen2009114–115,_119–121,_138-456]

Modern sporting was introduced to China from the West during the late 19th and early 20th centuries. [China first participated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_at_the_1932_Summer_Olympics) in the [Olympic Games](22576.md) in 1932.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhang2023a57-457] Although [association football](10568.md) draws large audiences, the [men's national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_national_football_team) has only qualified once for the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md);[^cite_note-458] the country hosted the [FIFA Women's World Cup](244862.md) twice and the [women's team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_women's_national_football_team) finished as runners-up once.[^cite_note-459]

China began to invest heavily in [elite sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-performance_sport) during the 1990s, and in 2008 [Beijing hosted the Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZheng_et_al.2018473–474-460] Elite sports in China are managed by the [General Administration of Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Administration_of_Sport_of_China), which oversees management centers for various sports, as well as provincial sports committees and teams. The vast majority of sports funding, especially for elite sports, comes from the government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZheng_et_al.2018476–478-461] China hosted the [Winter Olympics in 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Olympics),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMeng2023309-462] and the [Asian Games](39205.md) in [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Asian_Games), [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Asian_Games), and [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Asian_Games).[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYuan2023279-465] China is dominant among international competition in [table tennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_tennis_in_China) (often dubbed China's [national sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_sport)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhangZhou2019244-466][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEskiciogluTauscher20255-467] [badminton](3956.md), [diving](8402.md), and [weightlifting](37894764.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEskiciogluTauscher20255-467][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChu202451-468] and is among the top global competitors in [snooker](27378.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarris2020235-469]

Various card, dice, and board games gained popularity in imperial China, most notably *[weiqi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_\(game\))* (also known as Go) and *[xiangqi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiangqi)* (a relative of [chess](5134.md)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELiboriussenMartin2016227-470] [Mahjong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahjong) emerged during the 1800s, initially one of many popular [gambling games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gambling).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeinz20214-471] China is the world's largest video game market. Video games were introduced from Japan in the 1980s, and online video games grew to immense popularity alongside the spread of the internet.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETaiLu2021206–207-472]

The total area of China is listed as 9,572,900 km<sup>2</sup> (3,696,100 sq mi) by the *[Encyclopædia Britannica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica)*.<sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-britannica-9)</sup>

[^cite_note-15]: &#x20;GDP figures exclude Taiwan, Hong Kong, and Macau.

[^cite_note-19]: &#x20;The [Hong Kong dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_dollar) is used in Hong Kong and Macau, while the [Macanese pataca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macanese_pataca) is used in Macau only.

[^cite_note-20]: &#x20;[Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 中国; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Zhōngguó*

[^cite_note-21]: &#x20;[Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 中华人民共和国; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Zhōnghuá Rénmín Gònghéguó*

[^cite_note-23]: &#x20;Not including the *de jure* [Taiwan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_\(People's_Republic_of_China_province\)), controlled by the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China).<sup>[\[15\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZhao202346-22)</sup>

[^cite_note-area-24]: &#x20;The total area ranking relative to the [United States](3434750.md) depends on the measurement of total areas. See [list of countries and dependencies by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area) for information. The following two primary sources represent the range of estimates of China's and the United States' total areas. The *[Encyclopædia Britannica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica)* lists China as the third-largest country (after Russia and Canada) with a total area of 9,572,900 km<sup>2</sup>.<sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-britannica-9)</sup> The [CIA *World Factbook*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Factbook) lists China as the fourth-largest country (after Russia, Canada and the United States) with a total area of 9,596,960 km<sup>2</sup>.<sup>[\[10\]](#cite_note-CIA-12)</sup>

[^cite_note-30]: &#x20;An early use is on the [ritual bronze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritual_bronze) vessel [He zun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/He_zun), where it apparently refers to only the [Shang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shang_dynasty)'s immediate demesne conquered by the [Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhou_dynasty).<sup>[\[20\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChen2004198-29)</sup>

[^cite_note-53]: &#x20;Although 206 BCE is the generally accepted date for the founding of the dynasty, as it was when founding emperor [Liu Bang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liu_Bang) became the King of Han, he did not declare himself emperor until 202 BCE, after the [Chu–Han Contention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chu%E2%80%93Han_Contention). Han rule was interrupted by the usurper emperor [Wang Mang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Mang), who ruled from 9 CE to 23 CE.<sup>[\[42\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilkinson2012704-52)</sup>

[^cite_note-121]: &#x20;Constitutional amendments in the [First session of the 13th National People's Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_session_of_the_13th_National_People's_Congress) saw the removal of term limits for the [state president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_China), a ceremonial role. There have never been formal term limits for neither the [Chairman of the CMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Central_Military_Commission_\(China\)) nor the [General Secretary of the CCP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party).

[^cite_note-163]: &#x20;"People's Democratic Dictatorship" ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): 人民民主专政) is essentially synonymous with the more common Marxist term, [dictatorship of the proletariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship_of_the_proletariat).<sup>[\[150\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTELi2024205-162)</sup>

[^cite_note-177]: &#x20;Surveys which have shown high government approval ratings include 2003, 2009, and 2015–2016 surveys by the [Harvard Kennedy School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_Kennedy_School), and the 2017–2020 [World Values Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Values_Survey).<sup>[\[163\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJin2023136–137-176)</sup>

[^cite_note-TaiwanClaim-193]: &#x20;The People's Republic of China claims the islands of [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Taiwan) and [Penghu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penghu), which it does not control, as its 23rd province, i.e. [Taiwan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Province,_People's_Republic_of_China); along with [Kinmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinmen) and [Matsu Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matsu_Islands) as part of [Fujian Province](88585.md). These are controlled by the Taipei-based [Republic of China](25734.md) (ROC). See [§ Administrative divisions](#Administrative_divisions) for more details.
